
======================================================================
=== PLANTED QUESTION: lr_divergence_01 (learning_rate) ===
======================================================================
Research question: Does using an extremely high learning rate (100x higher than a well-tuned value) cause SGD to diverge rather than converge, compared to a well-tuned learning rate, when training a simple linear regression model on synthetic data?
Ground truth: supports

--- PLANNER OUTPUT ---
Hypothesis: Using an extremely high learning rate causes SGD to diverge rather than converge when training a simple linear regression model on synthetic data.
Task description: Using only Python's standard library, simulate 10 epochs of stochastic gradient descent (SGD) for a simple linear regression model on synthetic data once with a well-tuned learning rate and once with an extremely high learning rate. Print the loss value at each epoch for both.
Expected outcome: The run with the extremely high learning rate should show the loss diverging, while the run with the well-tuned learning rate should show the loss decreasing smoothly towards zero.

=== Attempt 1 ===
--- LLM COST (Coder) ---
{'total_duration_seconds': 71.416, 'load_duration_seconds': 0.146, 'inference_duration_seconds': 71.27, 'prompt_tokens': 469, 'completion_tokens': 403, 'total_tokens': 872}
--- CODE ---
import random
import math

random.seed(42)

# Generate synthetic data
n_samples = 100
X = [random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(n_samples)]
y_true = [2 * x + 3 + random.gauss(0, 0.1) for x in X]

def predict(X, w):
    return [w * x for x in X]

def loss(y_pred, y_true):
    return sum((y - y_t)**2 for y, y_t in zip(y_pred, y_true))

# SGD with well-tuned learning rate
w = 0.1
lr = 0.01
max_iterations = 10000

for epoch in range(10):
    y_pred = predict(X, w)
    grad = sum((y_pred[i] - y_true[i]) * X[i] for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples
    w -= lr * grad
    print(f'Epoch {epoch+1}, w: {w:.4g}, Loss: {loss(y_pred, y_true):.4g}')

# Reset parameters and repeat with extremely high learning rate
w = 0.1
lr = 100
max_iterations = 10000

for epoch in range(10):
    y_pred = predict(X, w)
    grad = sum((y_pred[i] - y_true[i]) * X[i] for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples
    if math.isinf(w) or math.isinf(grad) or abs(w) > 1e100:
        print(f'diverged at step {epoch+1}')
        break
    w -= lr * grad
    print(f'Epoch {epoch+1}, w: {w:.4g}, Loss: {loss(y_pred, y_true):.4g}')

======================================================================
=== PLANTED QUESTION: l2_weight_shrinkage_01 (regularization) ===
======================================================================
Research question: Does adding L2 regularization reduce the magnitude of learned weights compared to training without regularization, on a simple linear regression task with the same data and number of training steps?
Ground truth: supports

--- PLANNER OUTPUT ---
Hypothesis: Adding L2 regularization reduces the magnitude of learned weights compared to training without regularization on a simple linear regression task.
Task description: Using only Python's standard library, simulate one step of gradient descent for a linear regression model with and without L2 regularization. Print the magnitudes of the learned weights before and after applying L2 regularization.
Expected outcome: The magnitude of the learned weights should be smaller when L2 regularization is applied compared to training without regularization.

=== Attempt 1 ===
--- LLM COST (Coder) ---
{'total_duration_seconds': 95.883, 'load_duration_seconds': 0.169, 'inference_duration_seconds': 95.714, 'prompt_tokens': 454, 'completion_tokens': 607, 'total_tokens': 1061}
--- CODE ---
import random

random.seed(42)

# Data generation
n_samples = 100
X = [random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(n_samples)]
y = [x * 3 + 5 + random.normalvariate(0, 0.1) for x in X]
X = [[1] + [x] for x in X]  # Adding bias term

# Parameters initialization
w = [random.uniform(-1, 1), random.uniform(-1, 1)]
alpha = 0.01
l2_lambda = 0.1

# Step size
max_iterations = 10000

# Gradient descent without L2 regularization
w_no_reg = w[:]
for i in range(max_iterations):
    predictions = [sum(w_no_reg[j] * X[i][j] for j in range(len(w_no_reg))) for i in range(n_samples)]
    error = sum((predictions[i] - y[i]) ** 2 for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples
    gradient = [sum(2 * (predictions[i] - y[i]) * X[i][j] for i in range(n_samples)) for j in range(len(w_no_reg))]
    w_no_reg = [w_no_reg[j] - alpha * gradient[j] for j in range(len(w_no_reg))]
    if abs(error) > 1e100:
        print(f'diverged at step {i}')
        break

# Gradient descent with L2 regularization
w_l2_reg = w[:]
for i in range(max_iterations):
    predictions = [sum(w_l2_reg[j] * X[i][j] for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))) for i in range(n_samples)]
    error = sum((predictions[i] - y[i]) ** 2 for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples + l2_lambda * sum(w_l2_reg[j]**2 for j in range(1, len(w_l2_reg)))
    gradient = [sum(2 * (predictions[i] - y[i]) * X[i][j] for i in range(n_samples)) for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))]
    for j in range(1, len(w_l2_reg)):
        gradient[j] += 2 * l2_lambda * w_l2_reg[j]
    w_l2_reg = [w_l2_reg[j] - alpha * gradient[j] for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))]
    if abs(error) > 1e100:
        print(f'diverged at step {i}')
        break

# Output
print('Magnitude of weights without L2 regularization:', sum(abs(w_no_reg[i]) for i in range(len(w_no_reg))))
print('Magnitude of weights with L2 regularization:', sum(abs(w_l2_reg[i]) for i in range(len(w_l2_reg))))

import random

random.seed(42)

# Data generation
n_samples = 100
X = [random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(n_samples)]
y = [x * 3 + 5 + random.normalvariate(0, 0.1) for x in X]
X = [[1] + [x] for x in X]  # Adding bias term

# Parameters initialization
w = [random.uniform(-1, 1), random.uniform(-1, 1)]
alpha = 0.01
l2_lambda = 0.1

# Step size
max_iterations = 10000

# Gradient descent without L2 regularization
w_no_reg = w[:]
for i in range(max_iterations):
    predictions = [sum(w_no_reg[j] * X[i][j] for j in range(len(w_no_reg))) for i in range(n_samples)]
    error = sum((predictions[i] - y[i]) ** 2 for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples
    gradient = [sum(2 * (predictions[i] - y[i]) * X[i][j] for i in range(n_samples)) for j in range(len(w_no_reg))]
    w_no_reg = [w_no_reg[j] - alpha * gradient[j] for j in range(len(w_no_reg))]
    if abs(error) > 1e100:
        print(f'diverged at step {i}')
        break

# Gradient descent with L2 regularization
w_l2_reg = w[:]
for i in range(max_iterations):
    predictions = [sum(w_l2_reg[j] * X[i][j] for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))) for i in range(n_samples)]
    error = sum((predictions[i] - y[i]) ** 2 for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples + l2_lambda * sum(w_l2_reg[j]**2 for j in range(1, len(w_l2_reg)))
    gradient = [sum(2 * (predictions[i] - y[i]) * X[i][j] for i in range(n_samples)) for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))]
    for j in range(1, len(w_l2_reg)):
        gradient[j] += 2 * l2_lambda * w_l2_reg[j]
    w_l2_reg = [w_l2_reg[j] - alpha * gradient[j] for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))]
    if abs(error) > 1e100:
        print(f'diverged at step {i}')
        break

# Output
print('Magnitude of weights without L2 regularization:', sum(abs(w_no_reg[i]) for i in range(len(w_no_reg))))
print('Magnitude of weights with L2 regularization:', sum(abs(w_l2_reg[i]) for i in range(len(w_l2_reg))))

import random

random.seed(42)

# Data generation
n_samples = 100
X = [random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(n_samples)]
y = [x * 3 + 5 + random.normalvariate(0, 0.1) for x in X]
X = [[1] + [x] for x in X]  # Adding bias term

# Parameters initialization
w = [random.uniform(-1, 1), random.uniform(-1, 1)]
alpha = 0.01
l2_lambda = 0.1

# Step size
max_iterations = 10000

# Gradient descent without L2 regularization
w_no_reg = w[:]
for i in range(max_iterations):
    predictions = [sum(w_no_reg[j] * X[i][j] for j in range(len(w_no_reg))) for i in range(n_samples)]
    error = sum((predictions[i] - y[i]) ** 2 for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples
    gradient = [sum(2 * (predictions[i] - y[i]) * X[i][j] for i in range(n_samples)) for j in range(len(w_no_reg))]
    w_no_reg = [w_no_reg[j] - alpha * gradient[j] for j in range(len(w_no_reg))]
    if abs(error) > 1e100:
        print(f'diverged at step {i}')
        break

# Gradient descent with L2 regularization
w_l2_reg = w[:]
for i in range(max_iterations):
    predictions = [sum(w_l2_reg[j] * X[i][j] for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))) for i in range(n_samples)]
    error = sum((predictions[i] - y[i]) ** 2 for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples + l2_lambda * sum(w_l2_reg[j]**2 for j in range(1, len(w_l2_reg)))
    gradient = [sum(2 * (predictions[i] - y[i]) * X[i][j] for i in range(n_samples)) for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))]
    for j in range(1, len(w_l2_reg)):
        gradient[j] += 2 * l2_lambda * w_l2_reg[j]
    w_l2_reg = [w_l2_reg[j] - alpha * gradient[j] for j in range(len(w_l2_reg))]
    if abs(error) > 1e100:
        print(f'diverged at step {i}')
        break

# Output
print('Magnitude of weights without L2 regularization:', sum(abs(w_no_reg[i]) for i in range(len(w_no_reg))))
print('Magnitude of weights with L2 regularization:', sum(abs(w_l2_reg[i]) for i in range(len(w_l2_reg))))

======================================================================
=== PLANTED QUESTION: batch_size_gradient_variance_01 (batch_size) ===
======================================================================
Research question: Does using a larger batch size reduce the variance of gradient estimates (and therefore produce smoother, less noisy loss curves) compared to a smaller batch size, when training a model on the same synthetic dataset?
Ground truth: supports

--- PLANNER OUTPUT ---
Hypothesis: Using a larger batch size reduces the variance of gradient estimates and therefore produces smoother, less noisy loss curves compared to a smaller batch size when training a model on the same synthetic dataset.
Task description: Simulate 10 steps of stochastic gradient descent for a simple quadratic function using both a small batch size (e.g., 2) and a larger batch size (e.g., 10). For each batch size, calculate the average loss over the batch at each step. Print the loss values per step for both batch sizes.
Expected outcome: The loss curve with the smaller batch size should be more noisy compared to the loss curve with the larger batch size.

=== Attempt 1 ===
--- LLM COST (Coder) ---
{'total_duration_seconds': 63.41, 'load_duration_seconds': 0.118, 'inference_duration_seconds': 63.292, 'prompt_tokens': 481, 'completion_tokens': 238, 'total_tokens': 719}
--- CODE ---
import random

def quadratic_function(x):
    return x * x + 2 * x - 3

def derivative(x):
    return 2 * x + 2

def stochastic_gradient_descent(batch_size, learning_rate, max_iterations=10000):
    random.seed(42)
    x = 0.0
    for step in range(max_iterations):
        batch = [random.uniform(-10, 10) for _ in range(batch_size)]
        loss = sum(quadratic_function(x + dx) for dx in batch) / batch_size
        if abs(loss) > 1e100:
            print(f'diverged at step {step}')
            break
        gradient = sum(derivative(x + dx) for dx in batch) / batch_size
        x -= learning_rate * gradient
        print(f'step {step}, batch size {batch_size}, loss {loss:.4g}')

stochastic_gradient_descent(batch_size=2, learning_rate=0.1)
print()
stochastic_gradient_descent(batch_size=10, learning_rate=0.1)

--- CODE ---
import random

random.seed(42)

# Parameters
n_samples = 100
n_features = 2
learning_rate = 0.01
max_iterations = 1000

# Generate synthetic data
X = [[random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(n_features)] for _ in range(n_samples)]
y_true = [sum(x) + random.uniform(-1, 1) for x in X]
y_pred = [sum(x) for x in X]  # Initial predictions

# Initialize weights
weights = [random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(n_features)]

for iteration in range(max_iterations):
    loss = sum((y_true[i] - y_pred[i]) ** 2 for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples
    print(f"Step {iteration + 1}: Loss = {loss:.4g}")

    if loss > 1e100:
        print("diverged at step", iteration + 1)
        break

    # Compute gradients
    gradients = [sum((y_true[i] - y_pred[i]) * X[i][j] for i in range(n_samples)) / n_samples for j in range(n_features)]

    # Update weights
    weights = [weights[j] - learning_rate * gradients[j] for j in range(n_features)]
    y_pred = [sum(weights[j] * x[j] for j in range(n_features)) for x in X]

print(f"Final weights: {weights}")

=== PLANTED QUESTION: zero_init_symmetry_01 (weight_initialization) ===

Research question: Does initializing all weights to zero (instead of small random values) prevent a simple 2-layer neural network from learning effectively, due to symmetric gradients?
Ground truth: supports

--- PLANNER OUTPUT ---
Hypothesis: Initializing all weights to zero prevents a simple 2-layer neural network from learning effectively, due to symmetric gradients.
Task description: Using only Python's standard library, simulate one epoch of training for a simple 2-layer neural network with both random and zero-initialized weights. Print the loss values after each update step.
Expected outcome: The network with zero-initialized weights should show poor convergence or no improvement in loss, while the network with randomly initialized weights should show some progress in reducing the loss.

=== Attempt 1 ===
--- LLM COST (Coder) ---
{'total_duration_seconds': 74.631, 'load_duration_seconds': 0.598, 'inference_duration_seconds': 74.033, 'prompt_tokens': 450, 'completion_tokens': 498, 'total_tokens': 948}
--- CODE ---
import random

random.seed(42)

# Define constants
learning_rate = 0.1
max_iterations = 10000

# Initialize weights and biases
weights_random = [random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(3)]
bias_random = random.uniform(-1, 1)
weights_zero = [0] * 3
bias_zero = 0

# Define a simple neural network function
def neural_network(inputs, weights, bias):
    return sum(i * w for i, w in zip(inputs, weights)) + bias

# Define loss function (mean squared error)
def mean_squared_error(predicted, target):
    return (predicted - target) ** 2

# Simulate one epoch of training
def train_epoch(inputs, targets, weights, bias):
    for _ in range(max_iterations):
        predicted = neural_network(inputs, weights, bias)
        loss = mean_squared_error(predicted, targets[0])
        
        if loss > 1e100:
            print('diverged at step', _)
            break
        
        # Update weights and bias
        gradient_weights = [-2 * (targets[0] - predicted) * i for i in inputs]
        gradient_bias = -2 * (targets[0] - predicted)
        
        for w, grad in zip(weights, gradient_weights):
            if abs(w + learning_rate * grad) > 1e100:
                print('diverged at step', _)
                return
        
        weights = [w + learning_rate * grad for w, grad in zip(weights, gradient_weights)]
        bias += learning_rate * gradient_bias
        
        print(f'Random: Step {_}, Loss: {loss:.4g}')
    
    # Final random epoch update
    predicted = neural_network(inputs, weights_random, bias_random)
    loss = mean_squared_error(predicted, targets[0])
    print(f'Random final: Loss: {loss:.4g}')

# Input and target data
inputs = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0]
targets = [5.0]

# Train with random initialized weights
print('Training with random initialized weights:')
train_epoch(inputs, targets, weights_random, bias_random)

# Train with zero-initialized weights
print('\nTraining with zero-initialized weights:')
train_epoch(inputs, targets, weights_zero, bias_zero)

--- CODE ---
import random

random.seed(42)

# Define constants
learning_rate = 0.1
max_iterations = 10000

# Initialize weights and biases
weights_random = [random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(3)]
bias_random = random.uniform(-1, 1)
weights_zero = [0] * 3
bias_zero = 0

# Define a simple neural network function
def neural_network(inputs, weights, bias):
    return sum(i * w for i, w in zip(inputs, weights)) + bias

# Define loss function (mean squared error)
def mean_squared_error(predicted, target):
    return (predicted - target) ** 2

# Simulate one epoch of training
def train_epoch(inputs, targets, weights, bias):
    for _ in range(max_iterations):
        predicted = neural_network(inputs, weights, bias)
        loss = mean_squared_error(predicted, targets[0])
        
        if loss > 1e100:
            print('diverged at step', _)
            break
        
        # Update weights and bias
        gradient_weights = [-2 * (targets[0] - predicted) * i for i in inputs]
        gradient_bias = -2 * (targets[0] - predicted)
        
        for w, grad in zip(weights, gradient_weights):
            if abs(w + learning_rate * grad) > 1e100:
                print('diverged at step', _)
                return
        
        weights = [w + learning_rate * grad for w, grad in zip(weights, gradient_weights)]
        bias += learning_rate * gradient_bias
        
        print(f'Random: Step {_}, Loss: {loss:.4g}')
    
    # Final random epoch update
    predicted = neural_network(inputs, weights_random, bias_random)
    loss = mean_squared_error(predicted, targets[0])
    print(f'Random final: Loss: {loss:.4g}')

# Input and target data

targets = [5.0]

# Train with random initialized weights

train_epoch(inputs, targets, weights_random, bias_random)

--- CODE ---
import random

random.seed(42)

# Define constants
learning_rate = 0.01
max_iterations = 10000

# Initialize weights and biases
weights_random = [random.uniform(-1, 1) for _ in range(3)]
bias_random = random.uniform(-1, 1)
weights_zero = [0] * 3
bias_zero = 0

# Define a simple neural network function
def neural_network(inputs, weights, bias):
    return sum(i * w for i, w in zip(inputs, weights)) + bias

# Define loss function (mean squared error)
def mean_squared_error(predicted, target):
    return (predicted - target) ** 2

# Simulate one epoch of training
def train_epoch(inputs, targets, weights, bias):
    for _ in range(max_iterations):
        predicted = neural_network(inputs, weights, bias)
        loss = mean_squared_error(predicted, targets[0])
        
        if loss > 1e100:
            print('diverged at step', _)
            break
        
        # Update weights and bias
        gradient_weights = [-2 * (targets[0] - predicted) * i for i in inputs]
        gradient_bias = -2 * (targets[0] - predicted)
        
        for w, grad in zip(weights, gradient_weights):
            if abs(w + learning_rate * grad) > 1e100:
                print('diverged at step', _)
                return
        
        weights = [w + learning_rate * grad for w, grad in zip(weights, gradient_weights)]
        bias += learning_rate * gradient_bias
        
        print(f'Random: Step {_}, Loss: {loss:.4g}')
    
    # Final random epoch update
    predicted = neural_network(inputs, weights_random, bias_random)
    loss = mean_squared_error(predicted, targets[0])
    print(f'Random final: Loss: {loss:.4g}')

# Input and target data
inputs = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0]
targets = [5.0]

# Train with random initialized weights
print('Training with random initialized weights:')
train_epoch(inputs, targets, weights_random, bias_random)

# Train with zero-initialized weights
print('\nTraining with zero-initialized weights:')
train_epoch(inputs, targets, weights_zero, bias_zero)
